The plural `bubble_text_sources[]` array correctly carries `["text","toolFormerData"×7,"text","toolFormerData"]` (8 of 10 bubbles used the new fallback path; without 034, those would've been silently dropped from capture). Demo bar substring search works: founder's question phrase surfaces via ECHO. **But:** the captured atom contains only 11 of 26 bubbles in the composer — capture rate 10/21 = 47.6% (P1 territory per AC4 strategic-re-evaluation thresholds).
- **Sources:** Cursor SQLite `cursorDiskKV` composer `4f02b335-...` (fresh composer, founder's "where we left off" conversation); ECHO `echo.db` (1 atom captured for that composer); Cursor SQLite global state.vscdb for the eligible-bubble count.
- **Verdict:** 🟡 **partial pass — 034's two fixes work as designed, but a third gap (V1.5.7 streaming-continuation drop) is now structurally preventing the ≥90% threshold on agent-mode composers.**
- **Note (the diagnosis — multi-cluster pattern):** Walking the bubble layout chronologically: rowid 134495 (USER "use echo and undersntand...") → rowids 134501-134535 (11 consecutive assistants: 1 TEXT intro, 7 TOOL calls, 1 EMPTY, 2 more TOOL, 1 TEXT analysis "ECHO found two recent work threads...", 1 TOOL). 034's first cadence tick fired here; captured the 10 eligible bubbles as ONE atom. Then rowids 134542-134601 (15 more consecutive assistants — all still answering the SAME user question; the agent is mid-stream): 3 TEXT bubbles ("There are two overlapping threads...", "I'm appending a short dogfooding journal entry...", "ECHO says the latest real state is: `034` landed and was merged as `7362d88`...") plus 8 TOOL bubbles plus 4 EMPTY placeholders. 034's second cadence tick saw `lastSeenMap[4f02b335] = 70975526` (assistant); V1.5.7's fast-forward at `extractCursorTurns` lines 402-409 silently skipped all 15. **The verdict turn ("ECHO says the latest real state is...") was lost** — that's the "what did Cursor conclude" content that strategists actually want for cross-tool review.
- **Note (NOT a 034 bug; a known gap V1.5.7 left behind):** 034's Out-of-Scope explicitly preserved the V1.5.7 streaming-continuation fast-forward ("That fix stays"). The intent was to avoid scope creep. Empirically, V1.5.7 was designed for a different problem (suppressing 902K orphan_assistant_bubble warnings on already-captured turns where Cursor wrote streaming continuations). The same logic now defeats 034's intent on agent-mode composers, where partial captures + follow-on bubbles are typical. Filed as item 036 candidate in `backlog/_followups.md` under "From 034 merge" with three spec-shape options (continuation atom emission / hold-pairing-open / update-in-place re-emission — strategist lean: Option A, append-only-friendly).
- **Note (concrete content loss from THIS session):** Three TEXT bubbles in composer `4f02b335` carry substantive content that never made it into echo.db: bubble `e6676c74` ("There are two overlapping threads: the visible Claude terminal was mid-dogfooding item 034..."), bubble `b2f4f9e0` ("I'm appending a short dogfooding journal entry for this resume lookup..."), bubble `8265c39b` ("ECHO says the latest real state is: `034` landed and was merged as `7362d88`. The active..."). The first one is Cursor's analysis of overlapping work threads; the third is the **verdict turn answering the founder's question**. Both would be searchable via `search_memories(query=<phrase>, source_app='cursor')` post-036.
- **Note (workaround for AC4 dogfooding):** To get a clean ≥90% measurement on 034 alone, use a non-agent-mode Cursor composer (regular chat without tool calls). Each user message gets ONE assistant cluster → no follow-on bubbles → no V1.5.7 fast-forward path engaged. Single-cluster turns hit 034's demo bar reliably. The cross-tool spec review pattern (the load-bearing use case) is agent-mode by nature, so 036 is needed for the structural demo.
- **Note (035 merged at `a78e514` while this diagnosis was running):** Both M1-1 items now in `backlog/complete/`. 035's AC6 dogfooding (verify `tail_session(source_app='cursor', repo_path='/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo')` resolves to the correct composer) is a separate verification path — not blocked by the 036 gap, since `repo_path` resolution uses Cursor's workspaceStorage + composer registry, not the streaming-continuation extraction logic. Test tomorrow on a different juxtaposition (e.g., switch to Cursor's `isr-demo-mohsen` window briefly, then call `tail_session(source_app='cursor', repo_path='<Project_echo>')` to verify it returns Project_echo's composer not the global-MRU one).
- **Conjecture (observation-only):**
  1. **034 + 035 + 036 is the natural M1-1 trilogy.** Originally framed as "sub-gaps A+B+C" (cadence / parse / resolver), the empirical sub-gap D (multi-cluster streaming-continuation) is now the fourth. 036 closes it. The trilogy becomes a tetralogy.
  2. **The V1.5.7 fast-forward was an asymmetric fix.** It correctly handled "Cursor wrote more bubbles into an already-captured cluster" (silently skip — those continuation bubbles weren't new turns). But it also handles "Cursor wrote more bubbles after our partial capture, those bubbles ARE new content" — and silently skips those too. The symmetry-break is the bug. Item 036 should distinguish the two cases by checking whether the prior atom's `assistant_bubble_ids[]` covers the "expected" cluster or whether new bubbles imply a fresh continuation.
  3. **For V1 launch, the workaround (use non-agent-mode for dogfooding) is acceptable.** For the founder's actual workflow (cross-tool spec review = agent mode), 036 is load-bearing. Spec it next. **Predicted scope: 1-1.5d** (extractor-logic change, no new MCP surface, no storage changes). Builder agent: any (similar level to 034, possibly Cursor's Claude continuing the pattern).
  4. **The "data point N for item 031 dogfooding gate" counter is finally meaningful again.** Today's substring-search hit (`use echo and undersntand` → 1 match) is data point 5 of 5+ for dimension 1 (recovery primitive works). But dimension 2 (atoms exist for queries to hit) is now scoped to "atoms exist for cluster 1 of a multi-cluster turn." That's a partial close on dimension 2; 036 closes it fully.

Looking at echo.db, all 6 atoms for `32952945` have `workspace_id: ""` (empty) — Cursor hadn't bound this composer to a workspace's composerData registry yet when the atoms emitted, OR the founder fired Cursor in a window/workspace that hadn't synced to disk. 035's resolver reads composerData + workspaceStorage at query time, so a fresh composer that hasn't registered yet won't resolve via repo_path. This is a 035 freshness gap, not a 036 bug. Worth a follow-up data point for whether 035 should optionally fall back to "newest cursor activity globally regardless of repo binding" when the explicit repo lookup misses recent composers. Filed as a candidate observation; not a backlog item yet (need one more confirmation cycle).
- **Note (Cursor's review surfaced two findings worth bookmarking):**
  1. **Medium — extractor `workspace_id` emission path missing tests.** Resolver has tests but the extractor's `composerToWorkspace` binding path for the migrated-composer-registry shape (`selectedComposerIds`) is uncovered. Empirical correlate: this AC4 run's `workspace_id: ""` for `32952945` would be exactly the kind of regression these missing tests should catch.
  2. **Low — AC log-field name divergence.** Item 034's spec said the tick log field should be `tick_reason`; implementation logs `{ reason }`. Already in `_followups.md` from the 034 merge (line ~422), so this is a re-confirmation, not new.
- **Conjecture (observation-only):** the 6-atom continuation chain emitted in a 56 ms burst at a single cadence tick suggests Cursor's agent had ALREADY finished streaming all 69 bubbles BEFORE the daemon's next tick fired. The daemon then captured everything in one walk, segmenting it into 6 atoms via the natural continuation boundaries (each prior tick's checkpoint became the join key for the next). This is the "post-hoc multi-cluster recovery" path; the "live mid-stream multi-cluster" path (where Cursor is still streaming when the cadence ticks) is structurally similar but the latency between atoms would be 15s+ instead of milliseconds. Both work; a future stress test could verify the live-mid-stream case shows the same continuation chain shape.

Adjacent observation: an LLM doing cross-session resume would benefit from `search_memories` always emitting per-match `metadata.session_id` and `metadata.turn_index` in the projected metadata (it already does), so the scope-narrowing follow-up call is mechanically trivial. Current behavior is already correct on this front.

Today's exchange suggests `search_memories` over a same-day window is sufficient context-rehydration for the second voice; no need for `find_clusters` (which would have surfaced the same atoms but with extra cluster envelope cost). For "join a live strategy convo in another tool" use cases, two scoped `search_memories` calls + no `get_atom` chase is the cheapest viable shape.

Claude identified the deeper isolation leak: the wrapper's `ECHO_REVIEW_QUEUE_REPO_ROOT=$SMOKE_WORK` was defeated by slash-command bodies that hardcoded `cd ~/Desktop/Project_echo`; fixed at `aec75fd` by making codex/cursor/watch prompts cd through `${ECHO_REVIEW_QUEUE_REPO_ROOT:-$HOME/Desktop/Project_echo}`.
- **Sources:** source_breakdown={claude_code:10} for the tail search; exact JSONL `fs:/Users/zhenye/.claude/projects/-Users-zhenye-Desktop-Project-echo/d64c2d57-ec0a-45de-88dd-a7a05c866f59.jsonl`; repo filter `/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo`; local follow-up reads used `.claude/commands/review-queue-{codex,cursor,watch}.md`, `tools/review-queue/smoke-test-codex-runner.sh`, `.gitignore`, and the live smoke tmpdir.
- **Verdict:** ✅ right for the deeper isolation issue; 🟡 partial for final smoke readiness
- **Note:** After `aec75fd`, the smoke no longer ran in production: nested Codex operated inside `$SMOKE_WORK`, found the synthetic request, wrote `r1/codex.md`, and reached `commit-reviewer-response.sh`. The next failure is a smoke-fixture bug, not the same isolation bug: the smoke script copies `tools/review-queue/.` into a fresh git repo but does not copy `.gitignore`; because the production checkout has an ignored `tools/review-queue/__pycache__/_lib.cpython-310.pyc`, the fresh smoke repo accidentally tracks that bytecode file during `git add -A`. Running the Python validator rewrites the bytecode, leaving a tracked unstaged modification. Then `push-with-retry.sh` runs `git pull --rebase origin main` and Git refuses because the worktree is dirty. Root cause: AC5 smoke fixture construction copies ignored runtime artifacts without the ignore rules that would keep them untracked.
- **Conjecture:** Fix the smoke fixture by either copying `.gitignore` before the initial `git add -A`, pruning `__pycache__/` and `*.pyc` after copying `tools/review-queue`, or running validator calls with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. The cleanest production-safe fix is copy `.gitignore` plus prune runtime caches, so the smoke repo's tracked set matches production intent and `push-with-retry.sh` starts from a clean tree.

## Journal Operating Notes

- Entries with **only** a one-line note are fine. Don't gate on having a conjecture.
- Negative observations are more valuable than positive ones for V1.5+ planning. Don't filter.
- Screenshots / paste of MCP responses welcome — drop them inline as code blocks.
- If the founder forgets and skips a day, no problem. Quality of entries beats coverage.
- This file is not a backlog item — `tools/blocked.py` will not pick it up.
- Markdown is canonical. Regenerate `mcp-interactions-journal.html` after every journal edit with:
  `pandoc -s --metadata title="ECHO MCP interactions journal (cross-tool, cross-AI)" --toc --toc-depth=3 -H raw/internal/dogfooding/journal-style.html raw/internal/dogfooding/mcp-interactions-journal.md -o raw/internal/dogfooding/mcp-interactions-journal.html`

HTML twin regenerated. The file is explicitly marked "do not edit" — future comparisons go in sibling `role-typed-state-comparison-<spec-id>.md` files.

### 2026-05-14 01:38 PDT — Claude orchestrator diagnoses + fixes codex reviewer launchd silent failure
- **Trigger:** Founder pushed back on the "launchd is dead" diagnosis: "codex review was just working before and nothing changed. feel free to do some dry runs to confirm codex reviewer is truly broken." User wanted ECHO + dry runs to find the real cause.
- **Query inputs:** No ECHO MCP calls this tick (diagnostic was shell-based; the prior tick's `find_clusters` + `get_atoms` had already established no codex reviewer session was captured). Dry runs:
  - `launchctl print gui/<uid>/com.echo.review-queue-codex` → state=not running, last_exit=126, runs=100, StartInterval=600s, default PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin (no explicit EnvironmentVariables in plist)
  - `env -i HOME=$HOME PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin run-codex-reviewer.sh` → rc=1 with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'jsonschema'` from `_lib.py:18`
  - `/usr/bin/python3 -m pip list | grep schema` → empty; sys.path is Xcode-framework only (3.9.6, NO site-packages)
  - `/usr/local/bin/python3 -c 'import jsonschema'` → OK 4.25.1 (jsonschema IS installed, just on the wrong python3 for launchd's bare PATH)
  - `env -i HOME=$HOME PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/local/bin:... run-codex-reviewer.sh` → rc=0, wrote codex.md for 048 R1, pushed `cf0ec3a`
- **Returned:** N/A (no MCP). Output of the dry runs revealed the actual root cause.
- **Sources:** `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.echo.review-queue-codex.plist` (no EnvironmentVariables key — uses launchd's default); `tools/review-queue/_run_reviewer.sh` lines 28-40 (PATH augmentation on line 40, AFTER `python3 _reviewer_gate.py` on line 35 — the bug); `~/Library/Logs/echo-review-queue-codex.log` (no recent entries because failures happen BEFORE the `{...} >> $LOG_FILE` redirection block on line ~54 of wrapper); `tools/review-queue/_lib.py` line 18 (`import jsonschema`, added in commit `cd02160` for 039 cross-tool review dispatch); `/usr/bin/python3` (Xcode 3.9.6, no site-packages) vs `/usr/local/bin/python3` (3.10, has jsonschema).
- **Verdict:** ✅ right — the dry-run methodology surfaced the real bug. Founder's intuition was correct: nothing changed in the code, but launchd's PATH context apparently changed (likely `launchctl setenv PATH` got unset, or the 06:08:44Z success was from a manual kickstart in an interactive-shell context). The wrapper's PATH augmentation has always been ordered wrong; it only worked previously when launchd happened to inherit a fuller PATH from somewhere.
- **Note:** TWO observations.
  (1) **Real wrapper bug, applied fix:** moved `export PATH=...` BEFORE `python3 _reviewer_gate.py` in `tools/review-queue/_run_reviewer.sh`. One-line structural move. Under launchd's bare `/usr/bin:/bin:...`, the gate now finds `/usr/local/bin/python3` (with jsonschema) instead of `/usr/bin/python3` (Xcode no-site-packages). Verified by re-running the dry run with launchd-exact env: rc=0.
  (2) **The silent-failure mode is also a real friction.** Wrapper failures BEFORE the `{...} >> $LOG_FILE` block go to launchd's stderr (set to /dev/null in plist). No log entry, no journal entry. The next 10-min auto-tick fails identically, ad infinitum, until manually invoked from an interactive shell. Worth either redirecting stderr to the log file from line 1, OR setting StandardErrorPath in the plist to a separate `echo-review-queue-${REVIEWER}.err.log`. Friction-fix candidate for `_followups.md`.
- **Conjecture:** The 06:08:44Z success was most likely from a manual `launchctl kickstart` from the founder's interactive shell during 047 R3 cycle — kickstart inherits the calling shell's full PATH for that specific spawn, but subsequent cron-spawned ticks fall back to launchd's bare default. Founder may want to confirm whether they manually kickstarted around that time. Either way: the wrapper fix makes the cron robust to ANY PATH context.

### 2026-05-14 14:30 PDT — 049 FIRST FAIL-TO-CONVERGE CYCLE (load-bearing positive signal)
- **Trigger:** Founder asked Claude strategist to draft 049 (codex-skill-adapter — third sync target for tools/sync-skills.sh + vendor-neutralization of skills/review-pending.md) "with this shape and full auto mode with two codex reviewers till converge." Claude strategist drove the cycle through 8 cross-vendor review rounds; verdicts converged to proceed_after_patches both sides at R6/R7/R8 but reviewers (especially codex-ops's runtime/ops lens) kept surfacing new operational-safety findings each round; never reached zero-HIGH. Founder explicit decision at R8 escalation: ship with R8 patches inline + CLAIM-READY despite open HIGH, frame this as "the first fail to converge loop... a very good signal a responsible because the cross vendor multi reviewer has the discipline to reject convergence when the scope is too ambitious."
- **Query inputs:** No ECHO MCP calls during this journal entry (post-cycle observation log). Cycle itself used `wait_for_new_turns(codex)` + `get_atoms` repeatedly to monitor R1-R8 reviewer progress; those calls were journaled at their respective ticks.
- **Returned:** N/A — this is the post-cycle synthesis entry.
- **Sources:** 8 r<N>/{request,codex,codex-ops,combined}.md sets under `backlog/reviews/2026-05-14-049-codex-skill-adapter/`; 8 strategist disposition commits on `main` (`ce00143` → `f0bf4ea` plus post-R8 disposition commit); spec body at `backlog/ready/2026-05-14-049-codex-skill-adapter.md`; strategist task-state pointer; cycle round-finding tally: R1 7 (1H), R2 6→3-unique (2H), R3 3 (1H), R4 4→3-unique (1H, pushback), R5 6→4-unique (1H, pushback), R6 4 (2H, no pushback), R7 5 (1H), R8 3 (1H). ~20 unique-root findings total.
- **Verdict:** ✅ right — the SYSTEM did its job. The reviewer pipeline correctly refused to converge to zero-HIGH on a spec whose operational complexity (install helper + codex fan-out + copy mode + crash-safe staging + lock recovery + drift detection) genuinely exceeded what one cycle should carry. The discipline-to-reject-convergence is the property working as designed, not a failure of the protocol.
- **Note:** FIVE post-cycle observations.
  (1) **Each strategist disposition introduced new operational surface.** R2 catches my R1 patches; R3 catches my R2; R4 catches my R3; etc. Pattern: when reviewers find issues in my dispositions (not the original spec), that's the strategist over-reaching. Should have detected at R3-R4 and SIMPLIFIED (drop --copy mode entirely; ship symlink-only) rather than continuing to iterate. Logged as operating-model followup in `backlog/_followups.md`: cycle-length-budget enforcement — strategist decision-tree should detect "HIGH count not decreasing + new HIGHs from prior round's patches" pattern and force scope reduction before round N+1.
  (2) **Self-inflicted contradictions surfaced late.** R8's codex F1 HIGH was a self-inflicted contradiction from my R7 disposition: I added a `parse-failure preserves evidence outside RUN_DIR cleanup` test in AC3 that required an executable orchestrator the spec's Out of Scope explicitly forbids implementing. Reviewers caught what I introduced. Not their fault — mine for over-aggressive patch additions.
  (3) **Cross-vendor signal was strong throughout.** codex (procedural lens) and codex-ops (runtime/ops lens) caught DIFFERENT issues each round (R2: codex caught child sandbox + temp file race; codex-ops caught child sandbox + temp file race FROM DIFFERENT FRAMING — convergent. R4: codex-ops caught staging-inside-skill-root that codex missed. R5: codex caught JSON-contract under-specification; codex-ops caught lock-not-covering-symlink. R7: codex caught stdout-vs-output-last-message HIGH; codex-ops caught parse-failure-evidence-loss MED. Complementary findings, not contradictory. The cross-vendor pair was load-bearing for the discipline-to-reject-convergence property; same-vendor reviewer pair would have likely converged earlier on incomplete state.
  (4) **Verdict convergence ≠ finding convergence.** Both reviewers said proceed_after_patches from R6 onward (no pushback for 3 rounds) BUT findings continued. The protocol's verdict-divergence escalation criterion (proceed*/pushback boundary) wasn't tripped from R6+, yet the spec wasn't converging. Suggests adding a SECONDARY convergence criterion: finding-count-trajectory. If finding count not strictly decreasing across 2+ rounds with both reviewers at proceed*, that's a signal of fail-to-converge — distinct from verdict-band escalation.
  (5) **Founder R8 framing is the right reading.** "First fail to converge loop... very good signal a responsible because the cross vendor multi reviewer has the discipline to reject convergence when the scope is too ambitious." This reframes 049's 8-round-no-zero-HIGH from "cycle failure" to "discipline working." Wiki promotion candidate: document this as a load-bearing positive property of the protocol — `wiki/operating-model/fail-to-converge-as-designed-property` or similar.
- **Conjecture:** The pattern of "operationally-rich spec generates new safety surface per round" may be characteristic of install/CLI/operational-mechanism specs in general (anything that ships shell scripts running in user environments with FS state, locks, sandboxes, crash recovery). Future specs with similar shape (install helpers, daemons, cron infrastructure) should be scoped to single-mechanism-per-spec. 049 carried `--symlink` mode + `--copy` mode + stale-staging cleanup + stale-lock recovery + drift detection — should have been 2-3 separate specs. Mirror of 044's pattern (compound interest of infrastructure debt) but at the spec-design layer rather than the operating-model layer.
